## Exodus 33:3 (id: 11337)

* *land flowing with milk and honey:* Canaan was not as fertile as the Nile Valley in Egypt, but it was much more productive than the Sinai desert.
* *stiff\-necked people**:* They did not keep their promises.
* *I might destroy you**:* As God of the covenant, the Lord would enforce the covenant terms that required the destruction of those who violated it (see study note on 23:20–33; also see [24:7–8](https://ref.ly/Exod24:7-Exod24:8) and study notes).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Land Flowing of Milk and Honey (ID: `keyterm:LandFlowingOfMilkAndHoney`); Stubborn (ID: `keyterm:Stubborn`)
## Exodus 33:4–6 (id: 11338)

People might have worn jewelry and fine clothes while worshiping the gold calf. Removing them here shows mourning and repentance ([33:4](https://ref.ly/Exod33:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:4-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Stubborn (ID: `keyterm:Stubborn`); Mount Sinai (ID: `place:SinaiMount`)
## Exodus 33:7 (id: 11340)

The tabernacle is often called the *Tent of Meeting* (see study note on 27:21). In many passages, these two expressions refer to the same structure.

However, in this passage, the *Tent of Meeting* refers to a different, temporary tent. Moses set up this tent outside the camp before the tabernacle was built. 

Later, when the tabernacle was completed, it was placed in the center of the camp (see [Numbers 2:1–31](https://ref.ly/Num2:1-Num2:31)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:7
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 27:21 (ID: `11234@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
## Exodus 33:7–11 (id: 11339)

The way God communicated with his people before the completion of the tabernacle is likely described here. This helps explain the events recorded in [33:1–6](https://ref.ly/Exod33:1-Exod33:6) and [33:12–23](https://ref.ly/Exod33:12-Exod33:23).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:7-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Nun (ID: `person:Nun`); Joshua (ID: `person:Joshua.2`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)
## Exodus 33:8–11 (id: 11341)

*would stand … stand up and bow down:* The people considered it amazing that Moses spoke directly to God, "face to face" ([33:11](https://ref.ly/Exod33:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:8-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Nun (ID: `person:Nun`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Joshua (ID: `person:Joshua.2`)
## Exodus 33:11 (id: 11326)

Joshua had earlier gained experience as a military leader (see [17:9–13](https://ref.ly/Exod17:9-Exod17:13)). Now, he was gaining experience as a spiritual leader (see also [24:13](https://ref.ly/Exod24:13)). He may have stayed behind to guard the tent, a function the Levites would later fulfill with the tabernacle ([1 Chronicles 26](https://ref.ly/1Chr26:1-1Chr26:32)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Nun (ID: `person:Nun`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Joshua (ID: `person:Joshua.2`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)
## Exodus 33:12 (id: 11328)

*I know you by name:* God chose Moses himself.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
## Exodus 33:12–23 (id: 11327)

Moses begged God to go with him and the people to the promised land. In these verses, the word *Presence* usually translates a Hebrew word that literally means “face” ([33:14–15](https://ref.ly/Exod33:14-Exod33:15)). 

Earlier in the chapter, we learn that the Lord spoke with Moses "face to face" ([33:11](https://ref.ly/Exod33:11)). This phrase describes a close and direct relationship. It does not mean that Moses saw God’s physical face. It means that God spoke with Moses in a clear and personal way.

Moses wanted that same close relationship to continue. He did not want God to send only an angel or messenger. He wanted God’s own presence to go with them. Moses also wanted the people to share in that relationship in some way.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:12-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`)
## Exodus 33:13 (id: 11329)

* *please let me know Your ways:* Moses did not just want God's blessings. He wanted to understand God's nature and character. He also wanted to know why and how God acted. God's goal of revealing himself was starting to happen, at least with Moses.
* Moses wanted God to accompany them because they were the Lord's own people.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`)
## Exodus 33:14 (id: 11330)

*I will give you rest*: Literally *a place to roost,* as in a place where a bird can land and be at peace. Ever since Abraham left Ur, he and his descendants had had no such place.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:14

## Exodus 33:15–16 (id: 11331)

Moses made his request more direct. He showed he understood God's actions. The exodus was not just about leaving Egypt for Canaan. It was about experiencing God personally, which would change their lives. Without this, staying at Mount Sinai would have been better. Going to Canaan without the Lord would be disastrous.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:15-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`)
## Exodus 33:17 (id: 11332)

The Lord reaffirmed his agreement to go with them and his personal appointment of Moses.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
## Exodus 33:18 (id: 11333)

Moses asked for confirmation of God’s promises. He asked to see God’s glorious presence (literally *glory*). He was asking for an experience of seeing the very essence of God (see study note on 16:7).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:18
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 16:7 (ID: `11091@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`)
## Exodus 33:19 (id: 11334)

* In his positive reply, God changed the terms of the request Moses made. God promised Moses a glorious experience, but he would not let Moses see his face. In the Old Testament, seeing God’s face means seeing God fully and directly.

    Instead, God said he would show Moses his goodness. He would also proclaim his name. In the Bible, a name often expresses a person’s character and identity. God’s character is shown especially in his mercy and compassion (see [Romans 9:14–16](https://ref.ly/Rom9:14-Rom9:16)).

* *the LORD*: In many English Bibles, the word LORD (written in capital letters) stands for the personal name of God, *Yahweh*.

    In this verse, God emphasizes his personal name. This name shows that he is not distant. He is the covenant God (the God who makes promises) of his people.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
## Exodus 33:20–23 (id: 11336)

God desires to reveal his character to his people. However, to see his face means to see his full being (his true and complete nature). No human can see God in this way and live.

God’s glorious presence is described in [33:22](https://ref.ly/Exod33:22). That kind of direct encounter would bring death to a human person. God allowed Moses to see only a small part of his glory. Moses was given a brief glimpse, but nothing more.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:20-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
## Exodus 34:1–3 (id: 11343)

God called Moses to come back up Mount Sinai with two new stone tablets. The restrictions for the rest of the people are like those made at first (see [19:12–13](https://ref.ly/Exod19:12-Exod19:13), [21–25](https://ref.ly/Exod19:21-Exod19:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Mount Sinai (ID: `place:SinaiMount`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Tablet (ID: `realia:Tablet`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)
## Exodus 34:1–35 (id: 11342)

God granted the request of Moses ([33:18](https://ref.ly/Exod33:18)). He showed Moses his goodness ([34:1–9](https://ref.ly/Exod34:1-Exod34:9)). He renewed the covenant ([34:10–35](https://ref.ly/Exod34:10-Exod34:35)). 

This renewal was a statement made by God alone. God promised to stay with his people, keeping his covenant promises even though they had broken the covenant and deserved death and abandonment ([32:1–6](https://ref.ly/Exod32:1-Exod32:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:1-35

## Exodus 34:5–6 (id: 11355)

*the LORD*: This represents the personal name of God, *Yahweh*. This name appears five times in this passage. By repeating this name, God emphasizes his personal name. This shows that he is speaking and acting as the covenant God (the God who makes promises) of his people.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:5-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Generous (ID: `keyterm:Generous`); Compassionate (ID: `keyterm:Compassionate`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`)
## Exodus 34:5–9 (id: 11356)

Moses experienced God’s presence in a revelation of the name*,* or character, of God. As God had promised ([33:19](https://ref.ly/Exod33:19)), he showed Moses the glory of his goodness.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:5-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Compassionate (ID: `keyterm:Compassionate`); Generous (ID: `keyterm:Generous`); Pronounce Innocent (ID: `keyterm:PronounceInnocent`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Stubborn (ID: `keyterm:Stubborn`)
## Exodus 34:6 (id: 11357)

compassion and mercy (see [20:5–6](https://ref.ly/Exod20:5-Exod20:6); [33:19](https://ref.ly/Exod33:19) and study notes): The Creator who was revealing himself to the Israelites, and through them to the world, is a God of grace. Neither his justice nor his sovereignty are underlined here, as true as those attributes are. If God were merely just, the Old Testament would have ended at this point, or it would have picked up and started again with an entirely new family. This did not happen because God, in his unique character, is slow to anger and full of unfailing love and faithfulness. These qualities were the basis for his renewal of the covenant.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:6
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 20:5-6 (ID: `11155@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Generous (ID: `keyterm:Generous`); Compassionate (ID: `keyterm:Compassionate`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
## Exodus 34:7 (id: 11358)

God’s unfailing love and his generous desire to forgive are not weakness or indecisiveness, nor are they reason to sin. Sin will have its effects, because God created a world of cause and effect. The murderer may repent, be forgiven, and lead a new life, but the effects of previous choices will continue to play out. We should not sin just because we know that God will forgive (see study note on 20:5–6). • a thousand generations: See [Deut 7:9–11](https://ref.ly/Deut7:9-Deut7:11). • I lay the sins of the parents: Our sins affect future generations of descendants, but God restricts the natural effect of those sins to three or four generations. See also [Exod 20:5–6](https://ref.ly/Exod20:5-Exod20:6).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:7
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 20:5-6 (ID: `11155@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Pronounce Innocent (ID: `keyterm:PronounceInnocent`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)
## Exodus 34:8–9 (id: 11359)

Since the Lord had already twice promised to go with them ([33:14](https://ref.ly/Exod33:14), [17](https://ref.ly/Exod33:17)), this renewed request seems to show a lack of faith. It may also be that God’s presence had driven home to Moses how absolutely holy God is and how very different he is from this stubborn and rebellious people. Here Moses took his request a step further and asked God to travel with the people and make them his own special possession (see [19:5–6](https://ref.ly/Exod19:5-Exod19:6)). Moses asked for the complete restoration of the relationship.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:8-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Stubborn (ID: `keyterm:Stubborn`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)
## Exodus 34:10–11 (id: 11344)

God promised to miraculously care for his people as he brought them into Canaan. This care depended on their obedience. In Hebrew, the word translated as *observe* also means "obey"([34:11](https://ref.ly/Exod34:11))*.* Unlike English, Hebrew does not separate hearing a command from obeying it.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:10-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Amorites (ID: `group:Amorites`); Perizzites (ID: `group:Perizzites`); Hivites (ID: `group:Hivites`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Canaanites (ID: `group:Canaan`); Hittites (ID: `group:Hittite`); Jebusites (ID: `group:Jebusites`); Heth (ID: `person:Heth`)
## Exodus 34:10–26 (id: 11345)

Some of the terms of the covenant are restated, particularly those that prohibit the worship of other gods and that describe the proper worship of the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:10-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Play the Prostitute (ID: `keyterm:PlayTheProstitute`); Slaughtering (ID: `keyterm:Slaughtering`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Jealous (ID: `keyterm:Zealous.2`); Feast of Weeks (ID: `keyterm:FeastOfWeeks`); Amorites (ID: `group:Amorites`); Canaanites (ID: `group:Canaan`); Heth (ID: `person:Heth`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Asherah (ID: `deity:Asherah`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Perizzites (ID: `group:Perizzites`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Hivites (ID: `group:Hivites`); Hittites (ID: `group:Hittite`); Jebusites (ID: `group:Jebusites`); Passover (ID: `keyterm:Passover`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Sacred Pole (ID: `realia:SacredPole`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Kid (ID: `fauna:Kid`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Sacred Pillar (ID: `realia:SacredPillar`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Unleavened Bread (ID: `realia:UnleavenedBread`)
## Exodus 34:12–17 (id: 11346)

Worship of idols was prohibited, particularly the idols of the peoples into whose lands the Israelites were going. Central to this restriction was the prohibition of treaties, or covenants, with these idol\-worshiping peoples. Not only would the making of a treaty involve recognizing those peoples’ gods (because ancient treaties would call upon the gods as witnesses; see study note on 20:1–23:33), but the very existence of the treaties would predispose the Israelites to accept the ways of their treaty partners ([34:12](https://ref.ly/Exod34:12), [15–16](https://ref.ly/Exod34:15-Exod34:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:12-17
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 20:1-23.33 (ID: `11137@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Play the Prostitute (ID: `keyterm:PlayTheProstitute`); Jealous (ID: `keyterm:Zealous.2`); Slaughtering (ID: `keyterm:Slaughtering`); Asherah (ID: `deity:Asherah`); Sacred Pole (ID: `realia:SacredPole`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Sacred Pillar (ID: `realia:SacredPillar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)
## Exodus 34:13 (id: 11347)

Asherah was a Canaanite fertility goddess who would continue to be a temptation throughout Israel’s history (see, e.g., [Judg 6:25](https://ref.ly/Judg6:25); [1 Kgs 14:15](https://ref.ly/1Kgs14:15); [2 Kgs 23:15](https://ref.ly/2Kgs23:15)). • The poles seem to have been fertility symbols.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sacred Pole (ID: `realia:SacredPole`); Asherah (ID: `deity:Asherah`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Sacred Pillar (ID: `realia:SacredPillar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)
## Exodus 34:16 (id: 11348)

Marriage with people who worshiped other gods was a problem throughout the Old Testament. God warned His people not to marry those who followed other gods (see [Genesis 24:3](https://ref.ly/Gen24:3); [Ezra 9:1–2](https://ref.ly/Ezra9:1-Ezra9:2); compare [2 Corinthians 6:14–18](https://ref.ly/2Cor6:14-2Cor6:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Play the Prostitute (ID: `keyterm:PlayTheProstitute`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Exodus 34:33 (id: 11354)

*he put a veil over his face:* While Moses was reporting what God had said to him, he left his face unveiled. Then he covered it, perhaps because of the fear its brightness caused during normal conversation ([34:30](https://ref.ly/Exod34:30); see [2 Corinthians 3:7–18](https://ref.ly/2Cor3:7-2Cor3:18) for the apostle Paul's thoughts on this event).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:33

## Exodus 35:1–40.38 (id: 11360)

Much of the report of the building of the Tabernacle reiterates what was said in [25:1–31:18](https://ref.ly/Exod25:1-Exod31:18). There is a change from the imperative mood to the indicative, and there are certain changes in the order. This repetition underlines the point that the work was done exactly as God had commanded (see [40:16](https://ref.ly/Exod40:16)). The people had tried to secure God’s presence in their own way with the gold calf, and the result was disaster. Now they were doing it in God’s way, resulting in blessing ([40:34–38](https://ref.ly/Exod40:34-Exod40:38)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 35:4-36:38

## Exodus 35:4–36.7 (id: 11361)

Unlike the creation of the gold calf, where Aaron demanded that one type of material (earrings) be given (see study note on 32:2–4), the people were again invited to bring a variety of gifts ([35:4–9](https://ref.ly/Exod35:4-Exod35:9)) as their hearts were stirred and their spirits were moved ([35:21](https://ref.ly/Exod35:21)). Perhaps because the variety of gifts meant that everyone could bring something and because the giving was voluntary, the people gave too much and had to be commanded to stop ([36:4–7](https://ref.ly/Exod36:4-Exod36:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 35:4-36:7

## Exodus 38:8 (id: 11367)

* The plans for the bronze basin were part of the instructions to the priests ([30:17–21](https://ref.ly/Exod30:17-Exod30:21); [29:38–30:38](https://ref.ly/Exod29:38-Exod30:38)). Here, however, the text reports how the basin was built, following a logical order.
* This bronze, which they could polish to look like mirrors, was probably of very high quality. It was suitable for use in God's Tent of Meeting.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 38:8

## Exodus 38:21 (id: 11365)

Since the tabernacle contained the ark of the covenant (also called the ark of the Testimony), it is called the *tabernacle of the Testimony*.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 38:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Ithamar (ID: `person:Ithamar`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)
## Exodus 38:21–29 (id: 11364)

The very large amount of metal listed here includes more than one metric ton (about 1,000 kilograms) of gold, almost four metric tons (about 4,000 kilograms) of silver, and two and a half metric tons (about 2,500 kilograms) of bronze.

This large supply shows how eager the Egyptians were to send the Israelites away. The Egyptians gave the Israelites whatever they asked for so that they would leave quickly (see [12:35–36](https://ref.ly/Exod12:35-Exod12:36)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 38:21-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Talent (ID: `realia:Talent`); Shekel (ID: `realia:Shekel`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Uri (ID: `person:Uri`); Ahisamach (ID: `person:Ahisamach`); Oholiab (ID: `person:Oholiab`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Tribe of Dan (ID: `group:Dan`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Bezalel (ID: `person:Bezalel`); Ithamar (ID: `person:Ithamar`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Hur (ID: `person:Hur.2`); Dan (ID: `place:Dan`); A Half Shekel (ID: `realia:AHalfShekel`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`); Embroidered Cloth (ID: `realia:EmbroideredCloth`); Curtain (ID: `realia:Curtain`); Craftsman (ID: `realia:Craftsman`); Purple Cloth (ID: `realia:PurpleCloth`)

Moses inspected the finished work and confirmed thateverything had been done just as the Lord had commanded ([39:43](https://ref.ly/Exod39:43)). ## Exodus 40:1–33 (id: 11387)

The report on setting up the Tabernacle begins with the Lord’s commands to do so ([40:1–15](https://ref.ly/Exod40:1-Exod40:15)) and continues with the report on how Moses obeyed those commands ([40:16–33](https://ref.ly/Exod40:16-Exod40:33)). • The repeated statement that Moses did everything just as the Lord had commanded him ([40:16](https://ref.ly/Exod40:16), [19](https://ref.ly/Exod40:19), [21](https://ref.ly/Exod40:21), [23](https://ref.ly/Exod40:23), [25](https://ref.ly/Exod40:25), [27](https://ref.ly/Exod40:27),[29](https://ref.ly/Exod40:29), [32](https://ref.ly/Exod40:32)) is in radical contrast to Aaron’s self\-serving statement in [32:22–24](https://ref.ly/Exod32:22-Exod32:24). God wishes to share his presence with us, but his holiness is such that this can occur only if we renounce our own efforts to secure his presence and gifts.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 40:1-33

## Exodus 40:2 (id: 11388)

In the Tabernacle, the divine King met with Israel to communicate his will to them. • the first day of the new year: The Tabernacle was set up one year after God announced the Passover and the start of Israel’s calendar.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 40:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)
## Exodus 40:3 (id: 11392)

The Hebrew word translated Covenant is ‘eduth (“testimony”), which refers particularly to the covenant stipulations. The written copy of these stipulations contained in the Ark would serve as a testimony to the covenant Israel had entered into with God.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 40:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Covenant Box (ID: `keyterm:CovenantBox`); Curtain (ID: `realia:Curtain`); Ark of the Covenant (ID: `realia:ArkOfTheCovenant`)
## Exodus 40:33 (id: 11389)

*So Moses finished the work*: This statement marks the completion of the work on the tabernacle. The long process of building and preparing all its parts had now come to an end. See study note on 37:1–29.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 40:33
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 37:1-29 (ID: `11362@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Screen (ID: `realia:Screen`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)
## Exodus 40:34–38 (id: 11390)

The climax of the Exodus occurred when the glory of the Lord filled the Tabernacle. God’s goal from the outset was not merely to deliver his people from their bondage, but to bring them into a relationship with himself. Their real need was to know God personally in their lives. Without that, the Promised Land would not be a land of promise. Now it would be, because the glory that had been on Mount Sinai was in Israel’s midst; it would not be left behind but would travel with them wherever they might go.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 40:34-38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)
## Exodus 40:38 (id: 11391)

the whole family of Israel: This description is reminiscent of the way the book began (see [1:1–7](https://ref.ly/Exod1:1-Exod1:7)). Jacob’s family had become slaves in Egypt. What a dramatic change had occurred! They were no longer slaves. They were now in a position to be a blessing to the earth, as God had promised to Abraham generations earlier ([Gen 12:3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 40:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
